koṇṭuputtamatatarkaḷintiyāviluḷḷa caivarkaḷ pōṉṟu koṭiya pāmpaiyum tēḷaiyum kūṭa evarum kollac cammatiyār.  ittakaiya aṉpuruvam koṇṭa makkaḷ tāṉ aṟpa viṣayattiṟkum tam pōṉṟa maṉitarkaḷai tiṭīreṉak kattiyāl kuttik koṉṟuviṭuvar.  pautta matataṉiṉ kaṭamai kaḷil mika mukkiya 8 kaṭamaikaḷil tiruṭṭu, poy, kuṭi, vipacāram mutaliyavaṟṟai nīkkal  eṉṟu kūṟappaṭṭiruntum iṉṟu avaikaḷ putta matatarkaḷiṭam peruki irukkak kāṇalām.  curukkamākac coṉṉāl putta matakkoḷkai kaḷai aṟintavarkaḷ āyirattil oruvar kāṇpatum aṟitu.  puttamatak kōyilkaḷukku vicēṣamāy peṇmakkaḷē pōyvarukiṉṟaṉar.  kaṇṭi puttakōyilil yām kaṇṭa kāṭci viyakkattakkaṉa.  kaṇṭiyiltāṉ puttaruṭaiya pal oṉṟu vaikkappaṭṭu irukkiṟatu.  intappal maṉitarkaḷukkuriya iyaṟkaiyāṉa pallaiviṭa mikap periyatāy irukkiṟatu.  airōppiya turaimakaḷ oruvaḷ itu yāṉai tantattiṉāl ceyyappaṭṭatu eṉṟu tam nūleṉṟil eḻutiyirukkiṟāḷ. yāṉ ippallirukkum iṭattiṟkup pōṉatum aṅkuḷḷa purōkitar kāṇikkai pōṭum paṭi kūṟiṉār.  yāṅkaḷ kāṇikkaip pōṭa maṟuttuviṭṭōm.  aṅku pala peṇmaṇikaḷum cila āṇkaḷum appalliṉ mēl pōṭa puṣpaṅkaḷum kāṇikkaiyum koṇṭuvantu koṭuttu viṭṭup piṉṉar appūvil ciṟitu pākamum peṟṟukkoṇṭu ciṟitu nēram muḻaṅkāl paṭiyiṭṭu pūjikkiṉṟaṉar.  ippallai maṭṭuntāṉ pūjikkiṉṟārō? illai! illai! akkōyilil pūjai ceytu iṟantu putaikkappaṭṭa oru purōkitariṉ camātiyaiyum putta matak kirantaṅkaḷ vaikkap
paṭṭirukkum puttaka nilaiyattaiyum mikap paktiyuṭaṉ vaṇaṅki varukiṉṟaṉar.  āṅkāṅkē purōkitarkaḷ niṉṟukoṇṭu kāṇikkaiyākap paṇam paṟittuk koḷkiṉṟaṉar. apputta matak kōvilil arumaiyāṉa cittira vēlaippāṭukaḷ niṟaintirukkiṉṟaṉa.  iṉṉum ilaṅkaiyiṉ palvēṟiṭaṅkaḷil uḷḷa puttamatak kōyilkaḷaik kaṇṭēṉ.  aṅkē cumār 4 aṭikku mēṟpaṭṭa puttar uruvamum iṉṉum eṇ ṇiṟanta ciṟiya vikkirakaṅkaḷum varicai varicaiyāka vaikkappaṭṭiruk kiṉṟaṉa.  ilaṅkaiyil eṅku pārttālum mañcakkāyācamtaritta putta canniyācikaḷē kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉar.  avarkaḷil palarukku puttamatattiṉ uṇmaikaḷ teriyavillai.  āṅkilaṅ kaṟṟa cila naṇparkaḷ veḷḷaikkārarkaḷ eḻutiya nūlutavi koṇṭē ciṟitu putta camayattai uṇarntu irukkiṉṟārkaḷ.  uṇmai
yāṉa puttamatam āṅku nilaviyiruntāl perum naṉmai viḷaintirukkum.  turatiṣṭavacamāy ilaṅkai putta makkaḷaip paṟṟiyamūṭanampikkai, pakuttaṟiviṉmai mutaliyavaikaḷaip pōkki cuyamariyātai oḷivīca nam cuyamariyātait toṇṭar
kaḷāy intiyāvilirukkum aṉparkaḷ maṟavātiruppārkaḷāka.  aṅku nām toṇṭāṟṟiṉāl nām etirppārppataiviṭa atikap palaṉ eḷitil kiṭaikkum.  putta matak kuruvākiya tōḻar tarmakantapiṣī pōṉṟa aṉparkaḷ nammai varavēṟkiṉṟārkaḷ.  eṉavē vēlaikaḷ muṭintatum nāṉ cukamē puṟappaṭṭu eṉatu ūr vantucērntēṉ.  yāṉ aṅku taṅki yirunta kālattil eṉakkup pērutavipurinta cuyamariyātait toṇ ṭarkaḷ aṉaivarukkum maṟumuṟaiyum eṉatu naṉṟiyaṟitalait terivittuk koḷkiṟēṉ.

- (FoauR 27.3.1932, 10.4.1932, 17.4.1932)
1.	( Indian printed Textiles.) 
	(“ The art of using pigments to ornament fabrics has an ancient origin. The discovery of a fragment of a madder - dyed fabirc at Mohenjo-Daro sticking to a silver vage, would led us to believe that the mardens process of dyeing cloth was known in india five thousands years ago” - Indian printed textiles. published by All India Handicrafts Board, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, Government Of India (Delhi) 1950. )

1.	Brahmi has been traced back to the phoenician type of writting represented in the inscripting of Meshu king of Moab (850.c) Dr. Scaff however in his remarkable commentary to the periplus of Erthrean sea claims a phoneician origin for the Dravidian alphapet (P.229) g.Elliat smith.
2.	“It is also shown that the these schalars were not mistaken who commenced Brahmi with the south semitic and phoneician script.  For there is much evidence to show that they were also derived from the script of “Harapa  and Mohenjo-Daro” - R.G. Hunter.
	The two Brahmi scripts, one of Northern and the other of Southern India are developments of Mohonjodaro script that of South India is the direct continuation and development of Mohonjodaro script by Dravidian people of South India. Several signs of Mohonjodaro scripts are found in the pre historic pottery of tinnevelly Dt., in rock inscriptions of Nilgiris and tombs of Hyderabad states - by Rev. H. Hears M.A., light in Mohonjodaro (New Reveiw July 1936).
